La fiche F-Droid observée listait Conversations 2.20.4+free à partir du 29 septembre 2026, avec permissions réseau et localisation foreground selon listing; cette investigation n’a pas extrait le manifeste 2.20.4 installé. Les libellés Apple/Google du client n’ont pas été archivés ni vérifiés, et les permissions caméra/micro/contacts/photo varient selon client/OS. Aucune mesure de Firebase/analytics/crash runtime n’a été faite. Pas de cartographie DNS/ASN active: le domaine choisi doit faire l’objet d’une observation passive ciblée. Pas d’étude vérifiée associant **Cellebrite/GrayKey/Magnet + OS/build + Conversations/OMEMO version + date**; la base SQLite du rapport ROS indique les artefacts pertinents, sans constituer une extraction forensic commerciale. Aucun guide LE universel, aucune réquisition nommée avec données réellement remises par le fournisseur retenu. Sources: [XEP-0384 0.9.1](https://xmpp.org/extensions/xep-0384.html), [XEP-0166 Jingle](https://xmpp.org/extensions/xep-0166.html), [Conversation hosting](https://account.conversations.im/), [privacy policy](https://conversations.im/privacy.html), [ROS OMEMO 2016 PDF](https://conversations.im/omemo/audit.pdf), [ROS Conversations 2025 PDF](https://conversations.im/2025_audit_conversations.pdf), [F-Droid listing](https://f-droid.org/packages/eu.siacs.conversations/).

### XMPP / OMEMO — matrice des métadonnées

| Métadonnée | Fournisseur (serveur de compte / groupe / upload) | Contact | ISP | Apple / Google | Autorités |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IP | CONFIRMÉ par connexion au serveur; logs/rétention inconnus | POSSIBLE appels ou upload direct | CONFIRMÉ | POSSIBLE données de compte/store distinctes | POSSIBLE chez serveur/ISP sous procédure |
| Téléphone | INCONNU; dépend inscription | POSSIBLE si communiqué hors app | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN au seul FAI réseau | POSSIBLE compte store indépendant | POSSIBLE si inscription/facturation existante |
| Email | PROBABLE/CONFIRMÉ selon serveur choisi | POSSIBLE par profil/message | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN | POSSIBLE compte store | POSSIBLE chez opérateur si collecté |
| Identifiant | CONFIRMÉ JID, resource/device et roster visibles au serveur | CONFIRMÉ JID communiqué | POSSIBLE par DNS/traffic, pas le JID en général sous TLS | POSSIBLE lien d’installation/store séparé | CONFIRMÉ si obtenu du serveur/contact |
| Graphe social | PROBABLE roster, presence, MUC membership et destinataires | CONFIRMÉ échanges directs/membres visibles | POSSIBLE corrélation d’IP/temps | INCONNU | POSSIBLE depuis serveur/terminaux |
| Timestamps | CONFIRMÉ transport/presence; MAM peut stocker | CONFIRMÉ messages/présence locale | CONFIRMÉ connexion | POSSIBLE événements push | POSSIBLE selon logs conservés |
| Volumes | CONFIRMÉ approximativement si TLS/serveur observe stanzas | CONFIRMÉ tailles/fichiers | CONFIRMÉ octets | POSSIBLE push/usage | POSSIBLE |
| Groupes | CONFIRMÉ au serveur MUC/MIX | CONFIRMÉ aux membres selon salon | POSSIBLE par patterns seulement | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN au seul store | POSSIBLE serveur/contact |
| Appareils | CONFIRMÉ OMEMO device IDs/bundles, session clients | PROBABLE liste de devices publiée | POSSIBLE empreinte TLS/réseau | CONFIRMÉ compte store pour appareil | POSSIBLE |
| Push tokens | CONFIRMÉ au service push et probablement app/backend | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN au correspondant ordinaire | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN | CONFIRMÉ chez FCM/APNs si utilisés | POSSIBLE via ordre au provider |

## Telegram — dossier individuel d’investigation (13 octobre 2026)

**Périmètre.** Telegram est un service cloud centralisé. Sauf indication explicite, « Telegram » ci-dessous signifie Cloud Chats/Groups, et non Secret Chats. Les bots, canaux, mini-apps, Premium et appels ont des frontières et tiers supplémentaires.

| Catégorie | FACT | VENDOR CLAIM | INDEPENDENT EVIDENCE | INFERENCE | UNKNOWN |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Architecture | Service cloud centralisé, pas de fédération/self-hosting; synchronisation depuis le cloud. | Telegram dit utiliser un réseau/datacenters qu’il possède et exploite; la politique évoque une infrastructure de datacenters tiers loués. | FAQ Telegram dit que le code serveur n’est pas publié et qu’il ne serait pas possible de vérifier le code effectivement exécuté. | Centralisation donne au service une vue sur comptes, routage, cloud chats et communautés publiques. | Composition actuelle des régions/backend, sous-traitants et séparation des rôles techniques. |
| 2 Protocole cryptographique | MTProto 2.0; chiffrement client–serveur pour Cloud Chats, Secret Chats E2EE individuels et liés à des appareils; appels ont mécanisme E2EE selon la doc. | Le fournisseur annonce AES-256 et sécurité des Secret Chats; code protocolaire documenté. | Revue académique du protocole existe (p. ex. analyse formelle publiée 2020); elle ne certifie pas serveurs ni déploiement actuel. | MTProto 2.0 ne rend pas les Cloud Chats E2EE: Telegram peut déchiffrer leur contenu dans son architecture. | Version précise des implémentations en production, paramètres effectifs, audit indépendant complet de 2026 et garanties pour tous formats. |
| 3 Création d’identité | Numéro de téléphone requis à l’ouverture; nom/profil et username facultatifs. | Telegram présente le numéro comme identifiant nécessaire au compte, configurable en visibilité. | Politique courante liste phone number/basic account data. | Numéro lie compte/appareil à l’opérateur télécom même si les contacts ne le voient pas. | Identité réellement vérifiée, noms supplémentaires, paiement Premium et identité des numéros virtuels. |
| 4 Contact discovery | Import/synchronisation de contacts optionnelle; recherche username et ajout par numéro; les paramètres contrôlent visibilité. | Télégram affirme que contacts synchronisés sont chiffrés/stockés pour retrouver les amis et peuvent être effacés. | Étude NDSS de discovery a crawlé 10% des numéros US WhatsApp et 100% Signal et relevé API Telegram révélant données sensibles même pour des numéros non inscrits; paper soumis 2021, versions alors mesurées. | Numéro requis et lookup créent un risque d’énumération, mais résultats historiques ne démontrent pas le taux de vulnérabilité en 2026. | Rate limits et champs visibles après protections actuelles; nouveau test autorisé sur comptes contrôlés nécessaire. |
| 5 Métadonnées serveur | Numéro, IP, appareils/apps, changements de username et autres métadonnées anti-abus peuvent être collectés; politique max 12 mois pour cette catégorie lorsqu’elle est collectée. | La politique limite la rétention de métadonnées citées à 12 mois et décrit cloud chats synchronisés. | Politique versionnée fait foi pour la déclaration, non preuve indépendante de la pratique réelle. | La plateforme peut relier graphe de contacts, groupes, sessions et activité via un compte centralisé. | Logs exacts, champs conservés par région, query/search logs, rétention pour messages et groupes. |
| 6 IP utilisateur | Serveur voit l’IP de connexion; politique liste IP comme métadonnée de sécurité. | Fournisseur dit conservation max 12 mois si IP/device metadata collectée; policy annonce possibilité de remise sous ordre valide. | Cour documents US 2025 cite les réponses Telegram aux autorités depuis septembre 2024 comme généralement numéro + IP du login le plus récent. | VPN/Tor masque l’IP d’accès directe mais ne rompt pas le lien avec numéro/compte et timing. | Historique IP par session, durée de conservation actuelle et éventuels logs non inclus dans 12-month wording. |
| 7 IP au correspondant | Réglage P2P d’appel; autorisé, IP directe peut être communiquée au pair; relay forcé l’évite vis-à-vis du pair. | API Telegram documente P2P et réflecteurs; paramètre « Peer-to-Peer Calls ». | Documentation développeur officielle, pas de pcap effectué. | Relais déplace visibilité au service au lieu de supprimer l’IP. | Quel mode est actif par défaut dans versions et si exceptions/régions changent les candidates. |
| 8 Messages | Cloud chat messages/media/files stored and synced; Secret Chat is E2EE and device-specific. | Telegram affirme stockage cloud chiffré; Secret Chat E2EE. | FAQ reconnaît distinction; formal analysis treats MTProto but no broad audit of current app/service. | Cloud text/messages can be available to backend and legal disclosure, while Secret Chat ciphertext should not. | Exact key custody and internal access controls for cloud chats; moderation/AI processing paths per feature. |
| 9 Groupes | Cloud groups/channels are not Secret Chats and not E2EE; very large communities and bots exist. | Telegram describes end-to-end encryption only Secret Chat; public content moderates under policy. | API/FAQ semantics and server-side searchable community support establish backend access. | Membership, public posts, administrator graph and messages in groups can be visible to server. | Detailed history retention, server-side scanning scope and content deletion behavior per group. |
| 10 Appels | 1:1 voice/video calls, peer-to-peer if setting/network allows, relay otherwise. | Telegram claims call encryption and offers IP-hiding P2P setting. | API docs define P2P and reflectors, but no call cryptanalysis or runtime capture in this phase. | Direct call potentially reveals IP to other party; service relay sees session metadata. | Whether all calls share same cryptographic protocol and metadata logging/retention. |
| 11 Fichiers | Files/media in Cloud Chats are retained/synced through service; Secret Chat media follows device-specific chat semantics. | Telegram calls cloud history accessible from devices without third-party backup. | Official FAQ and policy. | Cloud storage allows service-side access/deletion/moderation and legal process; Secret Chat attachment still endpoint-exposed. | Encryption at rest/key management, exact object regions and retention after delete. |
| 12 Multi-device | Cloud Chats synchronized on many sessions; Secret Chats are per device and lost on logout according to FAQ. | Vendor presents cloud sync as feature and secret chat as device local. | Official FAQ documents difference. | Device list links multiple endpoints to one number and creates correlation data. | Device session metadata, history window, linked web clients version and key distribution details. |
| 13 Push | APNs/FCM or other OS push paths are expected on mobile; notification payload behavior may vary. | Public client source and FAQ describe notifications/settings; privacy docs do not completely characterize all payloads. | No live push capture or current build SDK manifest review completed. | Push provider sees token/time and may see message preview if configured/notification content sent. | Per-platform payload encryption, token linkage/retention, current third-party SDKs and notification preview behavior. |
| 14 Stockage local | Test data from Telegram iOS 11.1.1 was found under `telegram-data/.../postbox/db/db_sqlite` and a media directory; WAL may hold transactions not reflected in the main DB. | Desktop/mobile apps expose local chat data/cache; vendor describes secret chats as device-specific. | Jaeckel et al. (2025) used UFED Touch full-file-system acquisition and source-guided BLOB decoding; they report the database data they analyzed was not encrypted at the app layer. iOS build, UFED version and phone lock state are unreported. [Study PDF](https://dfrws.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Forensic-analysis-of-Telegram-Messeng_2025_Forensic-Science-International-D.pdf). | Shows that endpoint artifacts from that build could be read after extraction; not that locked-device protection is bypassed, nor that deleted data survives. | Current db schema/media/keychain by platform, iOS Data Protection class, lock-state constraints, deletion recovery and present-day UFED/GrayKey/Magnet support. |
| 15 Sauvegardes | Cloud Chats live on Telegram cloud; Secret Chats are not cloud-restored; vendor says default system backups disabled in FAQ. | Telegram says secret chats are not part of backup and phones use cloud sync. | FAQ and client settings; no independent restore test. | A device’s OS backup may differ from in-app cloud storage and build/platform. | Current iCloud/Android backup exclusions, export files, encryption and logout recovery. |
| 16 Analytics / SDK tiers | Product has optional translation; chosen messages may be shared with Google/Microsoft; Telegram claims providers do not retain/use unrelatedly. Voice transcription option documented with provider in product FAQ (exact path/version-dependent). | Policy identifies these optional third-party processing paths. | Public source lets some SDK/client code be reviewed; no installed binary traffic measurement/SDK inventory this phase. | Features selected by a user can expose plaintext beyond Telegram client/server. | Complete analytics/crash SDK list, remote config and actual data payload/retention. |
| 17 Code source | Mobile/desktop clients and TDLib open source; server backend closed; API/protocol docs public. | Telegram offers source and claims client transparency. | GitHub repos are public; FAQ itself states server source cannot prove code execution. | Source allows client review but not confirmation of backend operation. | Every platform/client version current commit and backend code/security controls. |
| 18 Builds reproductibles | Telegram publishes a build verification guide for Android/iOS starting 5.13, calls it experimental. | Vendor says binaries from App Store/Play can be independently compared to GitHub source. | Instructions exist; no reproducible build run or current version comparison done. | Reproducibility is achievable only for exact build/channel/toolchain and does not cover server. | Current verified versions, result per store, differences in encrypted iOS frameworks and signed distribution. |
| 19 Supply chain | Builds require Android SDK/NDK, Docker/toolchains and platform dependencies; signatures/source releases are publicly inspectable. | Telegram calls client apps open source and verifiable. | No independent build artifact comparison completed; published guide explicitly labels workflow experimental. | Third-party libraries, updates/store account and uninspectable backend remain trust surfaces. | Current dependency audit, maintainer activity, signed-tag coverage, SBOM and supply-chain attestations. |
| 20 Infrastructure | Central cloud; policy says for EEA/UK user data stored in leased third-party datacenters in Netherlands, with Telegram owning/operating servers/network. | Official privacy policy. | Policy is source for claim; this review did not query current DNS/ASN/route/hosting logs. | Leased location does not show where all backups, global metadata or support copies live. | Current domains, ASN, CDN, anycast, cloud/region mapping, redundancy and third-party access. |
| 21 Juridiction | Corporate policy names entities/regions; legal reach depends user’s location, processing entity, infrastructure and process. | Telegram identifies operating entities and policies; exact applicant legal entity varies. | Official policy/terms are first-party claims; no contract or cross-border transfer audit. | EEA data storage in NL does not by itself immunize against all external legal process or disclose actual legal control. | Entity for specific account, beneficial control, subprocessors, government-to-government transfer and challenge practice. |
| 22 Demandes policières | Current policy says after valid judicial order confirming a suspect in TOS-violating criminal case, Telegram may disclose IP + phone; transparency available via in-app regional bot. | Policy and legal guide define policy; DOJ affidavit says US requests started being answered around Sep 2024, generally phone + most recent login IP. | DOJ affidavit is sworn case-file description; official transparency bot aggregates count, not data fields per matter. | Cloud-chat content may be technically available under backend control, but statement does not establish routine disclosure of plaintext. | Exact data returned in each US criminal request, contents disclosed, preservation practices, legal rejection rates. |
| 23 Affaires judiciaires connues | Delhi High Court copyright case Neetu Singh v Telegram (2022): Telegram filed a compliance affidavit and sealed data; the Court says it reviewed a chart with administrator names, phone numbers and IP addresses for some channels. Telegram said some channel data was unavailable after statutory retention periods. In Dec. 2023, the plaintiff said the delivered information contained only names and IPs, an unresolved apparent discrepancy. | The policy then published by Telegram described a narrow terrorism-suspect exception; Telegram also argued that Singapore privacy law barred disclosure. Current policy now describes possible phone/IP disclosure under qualifying criminal-case orders. | Delhi High Court orders of 30 Aug. and 24 Nov. 2022 plus follow-up of 1 Dec. 2023; the Nov. order confirms the sealed production and categories the judge observed. [30 Aug. order](https://dhccaseinfo.nic.in/jupload/dhc/PMS/judgement/31-08-2022/PMS30082022SC2822020_121640.pdf), [24 Nov. compliance order](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/119238688/), [1 Dec. 2023 follow-up](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/2402568/). | Confirms judicially ordered provider production and data actually examined by the Court and supplied to plaintiffs’ counsel; the order permitted—but does not prove—police disclosure. A civil copyright case is not a criminal-prosecution return. | Exact values/accounts and complete field set remain sealed; whether police received the data; criminal-case return fields; ordinary Cloud Chat text supplied in a specific case. |
| 24 Audits | No whole-product independent public audit of current Telegram client+backend located. Academic protocol analyses are not equivalent to product audit. | Telegram says experts are welcome and source/protocol public. | Academic analysis of MTProto 2.0 and client source are independent scrutiny, not release assurance. | Proprietary server makes deployment/data-flow claims harder to verify externally. | Current independent app/backend penetration tests, audit report scope, version/commit, fixes and retest. |
| 25 Vulnérabilités | Client security bugs/third-party advisories may arise; exact currently exploitable findings should be mapped to builds. | Vendor maintains security contact and bug reporting. | No current critical advisory with verified 2026 affected build included in evidence set. | Absence from this search is not evidence of no vulnerabilities. | Complete NVD/GitHub advisory inventory, exploitation evidence and patch rollout. |
| 26 Recherche académique | Work includes MTProto formal analysis, contact discovery/scaling and protocol analyses. | — | [All the Numbers are US (NDSS)](https://www.ndss-symposium.org/ndss-paper/all-the-numbers-are-us-large-scale-abuse-of-contact-discovery-in-mobile-messengers/); protocol formal analysis [arXiv 2012.03141](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.03141). Findings are scoped/versioned. | Research on an old API/build can motivate tests but not establish current exploitability. | Full systematic research inventory after MTProto 2.0 implementation revisions. |
| 27 Incidents historiques | 2022 disclosure litigation; 2024 policy shift and 2024–25 US data sharing; Durov arrest/intermediary cases concern company/legal context, not cryptographic break. | Telegram publicly describes policy changes. | Court orders, DOJ affidavits and current privacy policy are primary records; press reports contextualize aggregates. | Do not confuse legal data requests with a breach or E2EE failure. | Any unreported breach, security incident or secret-process disclosure. |
| 28 Gouvernance / société / financement | Private centralized company and founder-led governance; no federation. | Company describes mission and management in public materials. | Corporate registries, official legal terms and policy are separate evidence; independent governance structure is limited. | Concentrated service ownership means company policy, executive decisions and jurisdiction matter. | Audited financials, beneficial ownership, controls, independent oversight and staff access. |
| 29 Résistance à la censure | No federation; central domains, app stores, phone/SMS, network endpoints and payment/Premium can be blocked. | Telegram positions itself as censorship resistant and clients support proxies. | Protocol supports MTProto proxies; no systematic current country-by-country OONI test in this phase. | Proxy option may improve connectivity but relay can observe IP/timing and state may block stores/SMS. | Actual blocking rates and stealth effectiveness by country/ISP/build. |
| 30 Maintenance actuelle | Official clients/releases and API docs continue; version varies by store and platform. | Release notes / app pages. | Repositories, current policy and store listings establish active distribution. | Active development does not answer patch latency/security review. | Release-by-release signed commit and reproducible match; exact versions across regions/stores. |

### Telegram — audits, repositories, mobile, forensics et droit

- **Repository/builds:** official Android client [`TelegramOrg/Telegram-Android`](https://github.com/DrKLO/Telegram) and iOS [`TelegramMessenger/Telegram-iOS`](https://github.com/TelegramMessenger/Telegram-iOS) (fork/repository ownership differs by client); [reproducible-build guide](https://core.telegram.org/reproducible-builds) says supported from 5.13 and experimental. It prescribes exact release tag/build matching and APK/IPA comparison. No current build was rebuilt here. GitHub source is not a server audit: the official FAQ explicitly says published server code cannot prove what is running.
- **Activity/dependencies/CI:** repository activity, signed tag reach, current dependency inventory and active maintainer count have not been normalized across Android/iOS/web/desktop in this turn; mark unresolved rather than inferring from stars or commits. Client OSS does not cover Bot API backend, cloud server, moderation or hosted file storage.
- **Mobile:** [Google Play listing](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=org.telegram.messenger) and [Apple listing](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/telegram-messenger/id686449807) expose developer-provided Data Safety / App Privacy disclosures. Those are declaration surfaces; the actual manifests, APK SDK list, runtime analytics/crash traffic, FCM/APNs payload, permissions and contact upload were not independently inspected in this phase. Official FAQ says camera is requested upon user actions and describes source-based check, but that remains vendor explanation until build-specific analysis. No assertion here that Firebase/analytics is absent.
- **Forensics:** a 2025 peer-reviewed DFRWS/FSI paper now documents Cellebrite UFED Touch full-file-system extraction of Telegram iOS **11.1.1** data on iPhone SE/iPhone 7 test devices, followed by decoding of `db_sqlite`, WAL and media. It does **not** report the iOS version, exact UFED build or lock state; it does not demonstrate an unlock bypass, current-build extraction or recovery of deleted data. This replaces the earlier blanket “no versioned tool result found” wording only for this dated test. [PDF](https://dfrws.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Forensic-analysis-of-Telegram-Messeng_2025_Forensic-Science-International-D.pdf). Cloud chat also differs: data may be obtainable via Telegram/backend process rather than device bypass.
- **Law-enforcement evidence ladder:** (i) **technically available**: server-controlled Cloud Chat data, subscriber account and metadata; (ii) **officially declared**: privacy policy allows phone/IP disclosure upon valid order in a qualifying criminal case; privacy policy says metadata may be retained up to 12 months if collected; (iii) **actually documented**: in the 2022 Indian civil copyright case, Telegram filed account-identification data under seal and the Court reviewed a chart listing administrator names, phone numbers and IP addresses for some channels; plaintiffs later said only names/IPs were supplied and some information was unavailable. The record permits disclosure to police but does not establish a police transfer. A 2025 U.S. affidavit says Telegram began responding around Sep 2024 with subscriber details, typically phone number and last-login IP, but this is an agent’s description of current practice, not proof of the exact return in that case. 2024 aggregate transparency counts (900 U.S. cases / 2,253 users) were publicly reported from bot output, not itemized returns. No verified case here establishes that Telegram delivered ordinary Cloud Chat plaintext in a criminal matter. [Delhi HC order 24 Nov. 2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/119238688/), [follow-up 1 Dec. 2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/2402568/), [DOJ affidavit, filed 31 Jul. 2025](https://www.justice.gov/usao-ndil/media/1409806/dl?inline=).
- **Sources:** [Telegram FAQ](https://telegram.org/faq?setln=en), [privacy](https://telegram.org/privacy), [MTProto](https://core.telegram.org/mtproto), [reproducible builds](https://core.telegram.org/reproducible-builds), [NDSS contact discovery](https://www.ndss-symposium.org/ndss-paper/all-the-numbers-are-us-large-scale-abuse-of-contact-discovery-in-mobile-messengers/), [Delhi High Court order, 30 Aug. 2022](https://dhccaseinfo.nic.in/jupload/dhc/PMS/judgement/31-08-2022/PMS30082022SC2822020_121640.pdf), [Delhi High Court compliance order, 24 Nov. 2022](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/119238688/), [follow-up order, 1 Dec. 2023](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/2402568/), [US DOJ affidavit](https://www.justice.gov/usao-ndil/media/1409806/dl?inline=), [official transparency bot](https://t.me/transparency), [DOJ/US court case archive, Telegram seizure warrant 2026](https://www.justice.gov/usao-dc/media/1437596/dl?inline=).

#### Telegram — matrice des métadonnées

| Métadonnée | Fournisseur Telegram | Contact | ISP | Apple / Google | Autorités |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IP | CONFIRMÉ à la connexion; retention policy max 12 months if collected; US legal responses typically last login IP per DOJ affidavit | POSSIBLE appel P2P | CONFIRMÉ | POSSIBLE login/store/network metadata separate | CONFIRMÉ : observée par la Cour pour certains canaux dans la production civile indienne; police effectivement destinataire **NON ÉTABLI** |
| Téléphone | CONFIRMÉ (account registration) | POSSIBLE selon visibility/privacy setting | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN to ISP except carrier/SIM records | POSSIBLE account identity | CONFIRMÉ : noms/numéros figurent dans le tableau scellé examiné par la Cour en 2022; remise à la police **NON ÉTABLIE** |
| Email | POSSIBLE (optional/recovery) | POSSIBLE profile | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN | CONFIRMÉ Apple/Google account separately | POSSIBLE if registered/account metadata |
| Identifiant | CONFIRMÉ user ID, username changes, device/app metadata | CONFIRMÉ username/profile, groups | POSSIBLE correlation only | POSSIBLE installation account | CONFIRMÉ subscriber/account information if requested |
| Graphe social | PROBABLE cloud contacts/chats/groups/channels/administrators | CONFIRMÉ chats/groups/channel membership | POSSIBLE patterns | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN directly from store | POSSIBLE via account records, public channel data, device |
| Timestamps | CONFIRMÉ service processes cloud-chat delivery/session | CONFIRMÉ local message/time | CONFIRMÉ connection/session | POSSIBLE push events | POSSIBLE, scope/retention unknown |
| Volumes | CONFIRMÉ storage/routing sizes, not exact retained logs | CONFIRMÉ content/media size | CONFIRMÉ byte counts | POSSIBLE app activity metrics | POSSIBLE if logs/cloud data retained |
| Groupes | CONFIRMÉ server-maintained Cloud Groups/channels | CONFIRMÉ to members/admins | POSSIBLE by traffic only | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN from store alone | POSSIBLE service data/legal process |
| Appareils | CONFIRMÉ sessions/devices/apps metadata | POSSIBLE presence and profile | POSSIBLE fingerprinting | CONFIRMÉ account/device association | POSSIBLE last login/session data |
| Push tokens | PROBABLE/CONFIRMÉ integration-specific backend delivery | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN to ordinary contact | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN | CONFIRMÉ APNs/FCM token if used | POSSIBLE with provider legal process |

## WhatsApp — dossier individuel d’investigation (13 octobre 2026)

**Périmètre.** WhatsApp Messenger grand public: contenu personnel et appels annoncés E2EE sur le Signal Protocol, mais client/service Meta centralisés et fermés. Business Platform, interactions avec bots/IA, interopérabilité DMA et sauvegardes dépendent de réglages/chemins distincts et ne doivent pas être agrégés sans qualification.

| Catégorie | FACT | VENDOR CLAIM | INDEPENDENT EVIDENCE | INFERENCE | UNKNOWN |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Architecture | Service centralisé Meta, identification historique par téléphone; serveur distribue ciphertext, identité et metadata; app mobile et appareils liés. | Meta dit que le chiffrement s’applique aux conversations personnelles; politique décrit infrastructure et sous-traitants Meta. | Meta engineering explique l’interopérabilité E2EE appuyée sur Signal Protocol dans l’UE; backend/client complets non ouverts. | La centralité donne à Meta comptes, réseau de delivery, contact-graph signalétique et surfaces de sauvegarde/business même sans message clair. | Cartographie backend actuelle, segmentation par régions, différences exactes interop/bots/Business. |
| 2 Protocole cryptographique | Signal Protocol dérivé, X3DH/PQXDH selon implémentation/version puis Double Ratchet; Sender Keys pour groupes, E2EE media/calls. | WhatsApp affirme E2EE par défaut pour messages et appels personnels, hors frontières produit explicites. | Étude 2026 modélise WhatsApp Web et observe conformité aux modèles symboliques des exécutions capturées; vérifie authentification/confidentialité au niveau modélisé, trouve des différences non documentées au fork libsignal. | Propriété du protocole Signal n’est pas preuve d’équivalence complète à Signal app ni de sécurité de toutes features. | Version précise et algorithmes actifs pour chaque client/device/business/interoperability; audit de backend; détails des differences post-publication. |
| 3 Création d’identité | Numéro de téléphone requis dans flux compte classique; nom/profil facultatif; mécanisme de username pourrait réduire l’exposition à l’échange de numéro dans les nouveaux flux, sans supprimer l’identité du compte. | Politique dit numéro nécessaire pour fournir service et trouver les utilisateurs. | Play listing et help center/Meta policies; pas de test enrollment courant fait. | Une SIM réelle permet corrélation opérateur–compte et numéro facilite enumeration. | Déploiement global des usernames, exigences par nouveau type compte / parent-managed accounts / linked devices. |
| 4 Contact discovery | App propose retrouver contacts via répertoire et phone matching; autorisation OS facultative côté accès local, mais flux précis dépend mode et réglages. | WhatsApp dit utiliser les numéros de contacts pour fournir la fonction et ne pas partager sa liste avec Meta « pour ses propres fins ». | NDSS 2021 paper a crawlé 10% de numéros US; NDSS 2026 a rapporté enumeration de milliards de comptes puis indique issue de rate-limiting corrigée en collaboration. Deux dates distinctes, anciennes mesures ne prouvent pas vulnérabilité courante. | Téléphone requis et availability checks créent une surface OSINT; du hachage seul n’empêche pas lookup des numéros à faible entropie. | Release/endpoint actuel, rate-limit et champs répondus; test contrôlé post-fix, nouveaux usernames et privacy settings. |
| 5 Métadonnées serveur | Compte, téléphone, contacts upload/lookup, appareil, groupe, heure de connexion et données routage; WhatsApp dit ne pas garder un registre complet de qui parle à qui en routine. | Politique: aucune conservation normale des messages remis et des journaux de transaction; les logs targeted peuvent être collectés sur ordre PRTT valide. | Government guide en français détaille les informations de base et collecte prospective; Meta transparency fournit demandes par région/catégorie, pas trace complète des données livrées dans chaque cas. | E2EE ne chiffre pas identité, destinataire, horaires, group membership, IP de connexion ou logs légalement ciblés. | Journaux internes actuels, logs de routage/rétention par feature, volumes et audience analytics. |
| 6 IP utilisateur | Service voit connexions/IP; une PRTT peut déclencher collecte targeted d’IP/timestamps/type/sens de communication d’un compte. | Guide LE indique ces données pour un ordre valable; politique générale décrit traitements IP/diagnostics. | U.S. affidavit 2025 associe les PRTT records WhatsApp d’un compte à groupe/senders/volume/communications; underlying response not public. | VPN déplace observation IP vers VPN; numéro/compte reste un pivot. | Historique IP passif retenu et durée; relation entre service logs ordinaires et collecte prospective. |
| 7 IP au correspondant | Appel 1:1 peut utiliser P2P; “Protect IP address in calls” impose relais; appels groupe sont relayés d’après Meta. | Meta dit que le relais cache l’adresse IP au pair tandis que média reste chiffré E2EE. | Meta Engineering annonce déploiement du réglage et architecture; aucune capture faite ici. | En mode direct le pair peut obtenir l’IP, en relayé l’opérateur du relay voit les IP/temps. | Candidats ICE effectifs, exceptions et état par défaut selon versions/regions; comportement lors d’appel link/interop. |
| 8 Messages | Messages personnels 1:1 E2EE par défaut; service détient ciphertext pour remise temporaire, supprime quand livré selon LE guide; messages non remis supprimés après 30 jours. | WhatsApp/Meta disent ne pouvoir lire les messages privés E2EE. | Model-monitoring paper 2026 fournit une preuve indépendante partielle sur WhatsApp Web protocol execution, pas une inspection de backend ou binaire store-wide. | Un participant, endpoint, backup non chiffré, report par receveur ou notification peut exposer texte malgré E2EE réseau. | Les messages interactifs/IA, business, report flags, endpoints de backup/interoperability et exceptions spécifiques. |
| 9 Groupes | Group messages E2EE; serveur gère delivery et group membership. | Meta dit que groupes privés restent chiffrés; metadata de qui participe ou quand peut être nécessaire au service. | Research on Sender Keys and formal-model study; PRTT case records establish messages/group metadata available under law. | Participants peuvent exfiltrer/capturer et group key update/compromise changes trust. | Exact group sender-key implementation/version and membership metadata retention/server log fields. |
| 10 Appels | E2EE audio/video; WebRTC network can be direct or relay; group defaults relay. | Meta claims E2EE calls; Protect IP setting for 1:1. | Protocol design & help docs; no independent live packet capture/current call audit in this phase. | Media secrecy against server does not hide IP/timing/network endpoints. | Call signaling logs, selective recording/transcription, interoperable and business call paths. |
| 11 Fichiers | Attachments sent in encrypted message; client encrypts media before server delivery; business/cloud integrations can introduce other endpoints. | Help center says photos, docs, voice notes and location are E2EE between personal senders/recipients. | Signal-derived design public; no app binary/traffic test of all attachment formats. | Metadata such as sizes/time and media local preview/caches survive; recipient can save. | Thumbnail/index/key handling, AI scan boundaries, CDN retention, link/interop exceptions. |
| 12 Multi-device | Linked devices including web/desktop and companion devices; independent session keys/device synchronization. | WhatsApp says linked devices E2EE and account’s primary phone not required continuously. | 2026 academic monitor targeted WhatsApp Web specifically, finding model-compatible observed executions and differences from upstream libsignal. | Every linked device is an added endpoint/key and compromise surface; account management metadata exposes device set. | Cross-device history sync details, credential rotation, app version floor and sync artifact protections. |
| 13 Push | Mobile push via OS provider; push may signal new message and app fetches ciphertext; exact payload may vary. | Vendor says notifications do not reveal private content unless user preview settings expose locally. | No live APNs/FCM payload capture in this phase; current SDKs not inspected. | Apple/Google see token, app/device/time and network signals; lock-screen preview is user-controlled risk. | Push payload keys, token linkage/retention, SDK versions and regional behavior. |
| 14 Stockage local | App keeps database, encrypted message/attachment data or decrypted presentation/cache as needed; client/OS keys and linked devices matter. | WhatsApp relies on OS/device security; E2EE is transport/session guarantee. | Public forensic literature covers legacy Android/iOS builds; no contemporary commercial-tool test (versioned) established here. | Unlocked device/app process can access plaintext; disk encryption/device passcode governs seized locked case. | Current database schema/keychain/Keystore behavior and media cache by platform/version. |
| 15 Sauvegardes | Optional E2EE cloud backup exists; enable with password/key/passkey-style recovery as product supports; ordinary iCloud/Google backup historically has different trust. | WhatsApp says E2EE backup prevents WhatsApp/Apple/Google from accessing the backup if feature properly enabled. | Help/Meta engineering docs; not independently tested by restoring current builds. | Backup ciphertext protection does not repair plaintext already held by an unlocked endpoint; weak password permits offline guessing if format allows. | Default state per user/platform, passkey rollout details, metadata retained by cloud provider and recovery workflow. |
| 16 Analytics / SDK tiers | Meta apps integrate analytics/diagnostic/product and business services; store labels list categories. | Meta says private messages/contacts are not shared with Meta for its own purposes while some metadata/device/business service info is shared within Meta companies. | Apple App Privacy / Google Play Data Safety are developer-submitted disclosures; Google explains labels depend developer disclosure and have scope exceptions. No current binary SDK/runtime measurement made. | Labels, policy and code manifest can differ; neither store label alone establishes no collection. | Exact embedded SDK inventory, crash logs, attribution signals, data-flow per feature and actual Play vs App Store behavior. |
| 17 Code source | WhatsApp client app/backend closed source; upstream Signal `libsignal` is public but WhatsApp uses a fork. | Meta describes Signal Protocol implementation and certain open-source dependencies. | 2026 paper reports previously undocumented differences between WhatsApp fork and libsignal; no source of client fork available for public review. | “Uses Signal Protocol” does not imply client parity; auditor/runtime evidence increases confidence only on studied Web execution. | Current precise fork version, proprietary verification code and code review scope. |
| 18 Builds reproductibles | No official public reproducible client build process for WhatsApp established. | Store publisher does not claim public exact-match reproducible builds in sources reviewed. | Closed app plus platform signatures means independent source-to-binary comparison unavailable in this evidence set. | Store attestation/signing identifies publisher/channel, not source semantics. | Internal build provenance, SBOM, in-toto/SLSA attestations, independent reproducibility program. |
| 19 Supply chain | Meta maintains closed clients/backend; Android/iOS app stores and FCM/APNs are dependencies; libsignal-derived implementation not equivalent to public libsignal. | Meta bug bounty/advisories demonstrate external report path. | 2026 CVEs listed below show recent patched client issues; academic 2026 found fork differences. | Signed update from legitimate vendor can still change implementation; store policy and dependency operations matter. | Full SBOM, active maintainers, signed release/tag, dependency pinning, patch latency by platform. |
| 20 Infrastructure OSINT | Central global Meta infrastructure, CDN/object/region services and push; no per-domain DNS/ASN snapshot captured. | Meta says globally distributed infrastructure / service providers. | Public infrastructure docs and transparency only; this investigation did not query passive DNS or ASN history. | Anycast/CDN/region routing cannot be inferred from brand; WhatsApp server may be separate by feature. | Exact current hostnames, ASN, regions, transit/cloud vendors, routing, retention and backup copies. |
| 21 Juridiction | WhatsApp LLC serves much of world; WhatsApp Ireland Limited handles EEA/UK region per corporate policies; data transfers subject to applicable law. | Policy and LE guide identify entity/address and processing; Meta global companies support service. | Court files show US PRTT process; official policies are first party. | Entity and physical storage do not alone dictate which legal process applies. | Case-specific data controller, cross-border transfer route, US/Irish objection and retention orders. |
| 22 Demandes policières | LE guide distinguishes preservation/basic account/subscriber/PRTT/emergency and says messages normally not retained once delivered; legal targeted collection may begin prospectively. | Meta says it reviews and responds to valid legal processes, may challenge overbroad requests. | Official guide includes examples of available data; official transparency report counts requests and compliance. | Authorities can obtain metadata even where they cannot request server-held E2EE plaintext; endpoint and cloud backup are separate routes. | Per-case actual fields, preservation details, emergency edge cases, content from report messages. |
| 23 Affaires judiciaires connues | U.S. v. [case 2:25-mj-30673-DUTY / 25-CR-?] 2025 amended complaint cites court-authorized PRTT records for account, group, senders and ~125 communications, range/timestamps and media types. | — | DOJ complaint is a primary affidavit but underlying company return is not attached. It demonstrates actual use of WhatsApp-returned records at least for communication metadata/group activity; some content wording's provenance cannot be independently separated from other investigative sources. | Count/media-type and participant/timing data were available from records; do not claim WhatsApp gave plaintext body based only on this affidavit. | Exact fields provided by WhatsApp, prospective vs retrospective interval and whether every excerpted message body came from handset/recipient/other source. |
| 24 Audits | No publicly established end-to-end independent audit of all current messenger app/backend/crypto found. Trail of Bits’ 2025 report covers **Private Processing for AI**, not ordinary WhatsApp conversations: 28 findings, 16 resolved, 4 partial, 8 open in that review’s final fix-status table. | Meta commissioned the scoped review; report also records its own point-in-time positive observations about enclave processing and post-processing retention. | Full [ToB report with fix review](https://github.com/trailofbits/publications/blob/master/reviews/2025-08-meta-whatsapp-privateprocessing-securityreview.pdf); independent runtime/model research is separate and scoped. | Findings about Private Processing cannot be translated into a weakness or assurance for the main WhatsApp chat protocol; they do document insider/TEE, logs, metadata, supply-chain and transparency risks in the AI feature. | Current rollout/version, whether open findings were fixed after Aug 2025, and audit coverage of ordinary messaging/backend. |
| 25 Vulnérabilités | Meta advisories 2026: CVE-2026-23866 (AI rich-response arbitrary URL/custom scheme handling; iOS <=2.26.15.72 / Android <=2.26.7.10 listed range) and CVE-2026-23863 Windows attachment filename NUL spoofing before 2.3000.1032164386.258709. | Meta says no evidence of in-the-wild exploitation for these advisories. | Advisory primary source; current deployed versions/rollout not independently checked. | CVSS severity should be combined with prereqs/surface/patch status, not read as observed exploitation. | All current advisories and exact OS/app version mapping outside 2026 list; uptake on installed devices. |
| 26 Recherche académique | Contact-discovery abuse, Sender Keys, runtime monitoring of Signal/WhatsApp Web, and targeted prekey depletion research. | — | NDSS 2026 paper reports past number enumeration and says rate-limiting issue was resolved collaboratively; WOOT 2025 Prekey Pogo found targeted depletion risk affecting PFS/availability in tested builds; 2026 runtime paper verifies scoped model. | Research findings are scoped/time-specific; no proof of ongoing exploit after fix or E2EE general break. | Current prevalence/remediation, production code mapping, device/app versions on current channel. |
| 27 Incidents historiques | Number/contact enumeration, 2021 Facebook phone-number scrape, legal PRTT data and software CVEs are documented; these are distinct incident classes. | Meta says PRTT and platform protections evolve. | Peer-reviewed/publication and court/advisory evidence. | Historical phone exposure may enable social engineering even if account protections later change. | Breach notification details and whether current contact discovery has additional undisclosed issues. |
| 28 Gouvernance / société / financement | Owned by Meta Platforms, publicly traded parent with advertising business; WhatsApp operating entities under Meta. | Meta says WhatsApp retains boundaries on private messages while shares service/security/business-related data. | Meta privacy policy, SEC/court/regulator filings and EU regulatory actions provide external records; no internal governance audit here. | Commercial incentives and integration are relevant to non-message data; they do not by themselves prove E2EE deception. | Internal access controls, employee review policy, cross-company joins and product roadmap. |
| 29 Résistance à la censure | Central domains, app stores, SIM/SMS enrollment, Meta infra and push are blocking points; no federation. | Meta does not position WhatsApp as a circumvention tool. | No systematic country/ISP blocking study in this phase. | VPN can route around some IP blocks but not registration dependency, store removal or targeted lawful block. | Country-by-country service availability, registration bypass, protocol obfuscation. |
| 30 Maintenance actuelle | Official security advisories/releases continue through 2026; multiple client/platform channels. | Meta states supported versions and security patches via app stores. | 2026 advisory dates and app stores establish active maintenance; no installed-device patch survey. | Users on old versions remain exposed until store rollout/adoption. | Release cadence, percent patched, Android sideload/business versions and post-patch test. |

### WhatsApp — audits et recherche approfondis

| Organisme / date / version / commit | Durée | Scope / méthodologie / exclusions | Findings / sévérité / recommandations / correction / retest |
|---|---|---|---|
| Moustafa Said, Aurora Naska, Kevin Morio, Robert Künnemann, **From Specs to Apps: Verifying and Monitoring Models of Signal and WhatsApp**, preprint 10 sept. 2026 / révision 12 sept.; WhatsApp Web, commit/build de production exact non publié dans abstract. | 3 person-weeks pour modèle WhatsApp Web, instrumentation, fuzzing/expériences selon le papier. | Instrumentation du runtime réseau/cryptographic components; modèles Tamarin compatibles; validation observée vs modèle symbolique, abstraction dépend d’extraction fidèle des événements. Pas audit du serveur, de toutes plateformes, du binaire mobile ou preuve mathématique du déploiement global. | Observed traces conform to model under stated abstraction; vérifie auth/secrecy des components core; découvre différences précédemment non documentées entre libsignal upstream et fork WhatsApp. Ce n’est pas une vulnérabilité démontrée ni une rupture E2EE. Publication [arXiv 2609.11882](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11882); corrections/roadmap non identifiées dans le preprint résumé; retest inconnu. |
| **Trail of Bits, Meta WhatsApp Private Processing Security Assessment with Fix Review**, rapport final daté **26 août 2025** ([PDF canonique](https://github.com/trailofbits/publications/blob/master/reviews/2025-08-meta-whatsapp-privateprocessing-securityreview.pdf); l’index ToB le classe août 2025, 12 engineer-weeks). Six consultants; travail 3–28 févr. 2025 : 2 engineer-weeks design/threat model, 6 infra, 4 code; fix reviews 23–25 avr. et 22–23 mai (5 engineer-days au total), confirmation partielle additionnelle le 12 août. | Le PDF ne donne pas de commit/SHA client pour les cibles; il couvre clients iOS/Android, libs C++, TEE server libs/infrastructure, transparency. Les identifiants de correction `D…` sont des références internes de changement Meta, pas des Git commit publics. | Revue design + infra déployée dans le réseau interne Meta + code; accès sources, docs, hôtes/guests confidential-computing déployés. Révision manuelle, statique/dynamique, fuzzing interfaces host/TEE, vérification dépendances/CVE. Exclusions : canaux physiques/attaques de tampering non testés; extensions proposées non implémentées; Fastly OHTTP relay, Cloudflare transparency monitor, firmwares/drivers AMD/NVIDIA, analyse complète stack Llama exclus. C’est Private Processing/IA, **pas** un audit de WhatsApp Messenger ou Signal Protocol dans son ensemble. | Résumé et inventaire finding par finding ci-dessous. Le rapport dit avoir validé, pour l’implémentation observée, le traitement dans les enclaves et l’absence d’indication de conservation post-traitement; cela reste un résultat temporel/scopé, avec résiduels et exclusions explicités. [PDF ToB](https://github.com/trailofbits/publications/blob/master/reviews/2025-08-meta-whatsapp-privateprocessing-securityreview.pdf), [article ToB sur l’audit](https://blog.trailofbits.com/2026/04/07/what-we-learned-about-tee-security-from-auditing-whatsapps-private-inference/). |
| Meta Bug Bounty, CVE-2026-23866 et CVE-2026-23863, avis 2026. | Non publié. | Triage incident et correctif par éditeur, pas audit indépendant; versions affectées indiquées par plateforme. | iOS/Android AI rich responses pouvaient traiter une URL arbitraire et custom URL scheme; Windows NUL byte attachment extension mismatch pouvait afficher un type mais exécuter un fichier. Meta dit ne pas voir exploitation in the wild. Corrections: after listed version thresholds; aucune vérification indépendante du retest/rollout. [Advisories 2026](https://www.whatsapp.com/security/advisories/2026). |

#### Trail of Bits — inventaire des 28 findings Private Processing

Sévérités et états repris des tableaux de synthèse et de fix review du PDF. Les `D…` sont les identifiants de changements Meta rapportés par ToB, **pas des hashes de commit vérifiables publiquement**. « Résolu » désigne la conclusion du fix review, sans démontrer la version déployée en octobre 2026.

| ID / finding | Sévérité | Recommandation ou mitigation indiquée | Fix review / statut rapporté |
|---|---|---|---|
| WAPI-1 ACS key rotation peut dé-anonymiser | Medium | Rendre la configuration ACS cohérente entre clients (clé racine embarquée, dérivation à attributs, ou cache Fastly); signer et vérifier les preuves; éviter une rotation qui réduit l’anonymat. | Résolu : configuration/keys via Fastly et clé publique Meta codée côté client (`D71515247`, `D71773542`). |
| WAPI-2 ACS peut fournir des tokens aveuglés invalides | Medium | Fournir une preuve d’équivalence discrete-log et la vérifier au client. | Résolu : preuves servies et vérifiées iOS/Android (`D69822878`, `D69829118`, `D70011790`). |
| WAPI-3 rotation OHTTP peut dé-anonymiser | Medium | Configurer les clés HPKE de manière cohérente et authentifiée; utiliser un cache de configuration fiable. | Résolu selon rapport : clé/config via Fastly et signature Meta (`D71515247`, `D71773542`). |
| WAPI-4 ciblage géographique des clients | Informational | Séparer privilèges de changements clients/logs des privilèges de routage PoP→datacenter; randomiser durées/réutilisation des tokens pour réduire la corrélation. | **Non résolu** dans le tableau final; Meta dit évaluer les mitigations à l’échelle/steady-state. |
| WAPI-5 données sensibles d’inférence loguées côté client | Low | Détecter les formes de tokens et réponses sensibles dans les logs; alerter équipe produit et sécurité; contrôles dans hooks/CI. | **Non résolu**; Meta répond par principes généraux de code review/minimisation, sans correctif spécifique vérifié. |
| WAPI-6 attributs suivis permettant ciblage | Informational | Minimiser collecte/rétention d’attributs identifiants de temps, lieu, latence et usage; expliciter le risque dans le threat model interne. | **Non résolu**; Meta dit ne pas avoir observé de preuve que ces logs brisent la non-targetability. |
| WAPI-7 attestation sans fraîcheur | High | Lier l’attestation à un nonce client/TLS par session. | Résolu : `client_random` inclus et comparé (`D74603096`). |
| WAPI-8 TCB SEV-SNP non vérifié contre certificat VCEK | High | Vérifier les niveaux bootloader/TEE/SNP/microcode signés dans les extensions X.509 VCEK, pas uniquement les champs reportés par firmware. | Résolu : validation des champs VCEK étendue (`D69724419`). |
| WAPI-9 politiques Guest SEV-SNP non toutes appliquées | Informational | Imposer flags de politique guest stricts (debug, migration, SMT et protections disponibles). | **Partiel** : SMT désactivé au niveau hôte, contrôles client MIGRATE_MA/DEBUG; les autres flags n’étaient pas supportés par le kernel/KVM testé. Confirmation partielle datée du 12 août 2025. |
| WAPI-10 attestation non liée aux machines Meta | High | Restreindre l’attestation aux hôtes réellement contrôlés par Meta, par CA/provisionnement ou allowlist hardware ID vérifiée. | **Partiel** : enrollment trust-on-first-use et publication de digest VCEK; ToB dit que provenance/intégrité hardware ne sont pas entièrement garanties (`D73932854`, `D73827419`). |
| WAPI-11 TLS 1.2 et suites permissives | Informational | Passer à TLS 1.3; planifier le post-quantique pour résister au SNDL. | Résolu pour TLS : clients RA-TLS n’utilisent que TLS 1.3 (`D69931378`, `D70004593`); PQ traité séparément en WAPI-12. |
| WAPI-12 vulnérabilité SNDL post-quantique | Informational | Plan public de transition vers TLS et primitives PQ-hardened. | **Non résolu** au rapport final; Meta dit travailler à la transition et juge l’attaque moins probable avec ses protections d’anonymat. |
| WAPI-13 injection de variables d’environnement dans CVM | High | Parser/allowlister les variables et valider/quoting stricts, plutôt qu’accepter un fichier arbitraire. | Résolu : allowlist fixe et valeurs validées (longueur max 50, charset borné) (`D72583676`, `D72805813`). |
| WAPI-14 usage rsync non sûr | Informational | Éviter dereference symlink; `--safe-links`; séparateurs de fichiers NUL; éviter glob imprévisible. | Résolu : copie temporaire sans `-L`, fichiers séparés par NUL et glob supprimé (`D69858604`, `D71784233`). |
| WAPI-15 modèles chargés comme pickle | Informational | Utiliser un format sérialisé moins dangereux / chargement poids seuls, ou retirer le chargement pickle de production. | Résolu : migration de la pile vers vLLM, les `torch.load` concernés ne sont plus exécutés en production (`D70305933`). |
| WAPI-16 taille de sortie LLM non masquée | Low | Padding de réponses (p. ex. Padmé) et plafond de longueur pour réduire fuite par taille chiffrée. | **Non résolu**; Meta reconnaît l’impact faible mais conserve un travail d’endurcissement. |
| WAPI-17 hyperviseur injecte des ACPI SSDT | High | Lier les tables ACPI à la mesure de boot et vérifier leur digest dans CVM. | Résolu : bootloader Stage0 custom, SSDT incluse dans measurement/digest attendu (`D73804542`, `D73937575`). ToB recommande de rendre QEMU command/artifacts publics. |
| WAPI-18 absence de reproductibilité des images CVM | High | Construire artefacts depuis sources versionnées, contrôler binaires mis en cache et publier provenance/digests reproductibles. | **Partiel** : dépendances contrôlées/manifests et sources plus ouvertes, mais images pas encore pleinement reproductibles; audit tiers depuis source seule impossible. |
| WAPI-19 digests artifacts privés ne préservent pas l’arborescence | Low | Hacher un paquet qui préserve structure/noms/metadata (ex. tar) et ajouter un namespace de domaine. | **Non résolu** dans le rapport final. |
| WAPI-20 namespace de transparence non imposé | High | Épingler namespaces attendus dans client et orchestrateur; lier le type TEE au certificat. | Résolu sur la protection principale par restriction de namespaces (`D70708182`); ToB recommande encore de distinguer explicitement le type TEE via SAN. |
| WAPI-21 artifacts de transparence sans expiration | Informational | Version minimale côté client et durée de preuve limitée. | Résolu : expiration selon type; image CVM expirait après 3 semaines (`D70813884`). |
| WAPI-22 ports I/O inutiles accessibles à hyperviseur malveillant | Medium | Allowlist explicite des ports; ne pas exposer le chargement firmware/blobs non mesurés. | Résolu : allowlist noyau pour ports, hors mode debug (`D72410416`). |
| WAPI-23 échec vérification transparency peut être silencieux | Low | Faire dépendre le démarrage CVM de la vérification et tester chemin négatif. | Résolu : dépendance systemd; startup échoue si preuve invalide (`D70925617`). |
| WAPI-24 versions Mbed TLS/OpenSSL avec CVE | Informational | Mettre à jour les dépendances et suivre les divulgations, notamment side-channel local. | **Partiel** : Mbed TLS 3.6.0 sur composants concernés; OpenSSL restait non corrigé à la revue (Meta invoquait coût perf/absence d’exploit concret). |
| WAPI-25 protections Spectre absentes | Informational | Ajouter les flags noyau recommandés et désactiver SMT lorsque requis. | Résolu selon rapport : options mitigations ajoutées au kernel CVM (`D70646271`). |
| WAPI-26 fuite tokenizer LLM via cache side-channel | Informational | Réduire SMT; tokenizer avec accès mémoire moins dépendants du secret/hash rerandomisé, ou tokenisation côté client; étude dédiée. | **Non résolu**; aucun side-channel n’a été exécuté pendant l’audit; risque et capacité réelle restent à mesurer. |
| WAPI-27 transparency dépend d’une partie centrale honnête | Informational | Lier cryptographiquement les epochs et utiliser un journal append-only/Merkle vérifiable. | **Non résolu**; Meta envisage une vérification cryptographique dans une itération future. |
| WAPI-28 LLM GPU sans vérification attestation NVIDIA | High | Vérifier preuves GPU/attestation avant chargement du service. | Résolu : utilitaire NVIDIA `nvtrust` vérifie rapports avant lancement (`D70814218`); RIM encore embarqués dans build, provisioning/révocation runtime futurs. |

**Résultat agrégé ToB au 26 août 2025 :** 16 résolus, 4 partiellement résolus, 8 non résolus. Aucun des statuts n’atteste l’état déployé aujourd’hui. Les quatre « partiels » sont WAPI-9, -10, -18, -24. Les huit ouverts sont -4, -5, -6, -12, -16, -19, -26, -27. Cet audit ne doit pas servir à caractériser le chiffrement des chats ordinaires : il concerne une fonctionnalité distincte de traitement IA sous TEE.

**Recherche vulnérabilité protocol-level.** *Prekey Pogo* (USENIX WOOT 2025) décrit une attaque ciblée d’épuisement des one-time prekeys d’un appareil WhatsApp pouvant dégrader PFS pour certains messages et disponibilité/confidentialité sous conditions; cela n’équivaut pas à déchiffrer toutes conversations. [Papier WOOT](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/woot25-moratti.pdf). *Hey There! You Are Using WhatsApp* (NDSS 2026) a énuméré >100 M numéros/heure dans un test avant correction, réutilisation/prekeys multi-comptes observées; version mise à jour déclare que les auteurs ont collaboré et rate-limit sous-jacent corrigé. [NDSS 2026](https://www.ndss-symposium.org/ndss-paper/hey-there-you-are-using-whatsapp-enumerating-three-billion-accounts-for-security-and-privacy/). Ne pas présenter ces anciens états comme vulnérabilité active le 13-10-2026.

**Mobile/privacy labels.** [Apple listing](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/whatsapp-messenger/id310633997) et [Google Play Data Safety](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.whatsapp) sont les déclarations éditeur. Le Help Center répond à l’étiquette Apple et affirme que contact list n’est pas partagée avec Meta pour ses propres fins; Apple regroupe certaines catégories sous “linked to you”; Google documente que les développeurs soumettent les catégories et certaines collectes transitoires/non stockées sont hors-scope. Ce n’est pas une analyse binaire. Manifestes, permissions actuelles, Firebase/APNs/SDK list, camera/mic/location/photos permissions, diagnostics, contact discovery payload et crash runtime n’ont pas été mesurés dans cette phase. Sources: [WhatsApp privacy label explainer](https://faq.whatsapp.com/1455645071585052/?locale=fr_FR), [Google Data Safety methodology](https://support.google.com/googleplay/answer/11416267?hl=en), [Apple developer privacy disclosure](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/user-privacy-and-data-use/).

**Repos/builds/infrastructure.** Main client binaries, server and official apps are proprietary; protocol lineage is documented but no source-to-store deterministic build is provided. `signalapp/libsignal` is not WhatsApp’s complete fork. Full dependency inventory, SBOM, signature verification and active maintainer count of private repositories unavailable. Meta global infrastructure and partner disclosures are public at high level; exact WhatsApp domain DNS/ASN/CDN/regions were not passively snapshotted here. Store labels and policy cannot establish exact FCM/APNs payload or analytics SDK behavior.

**Forensics and legal evidence.** No reproducible literature result in this dossier names **Cellebrite UFED/PA, GrayKey or Magnet + exact OS version + WhatsApp build + extraction date** and proves present capability. Public forensic papers mostly concern older builds/OS; must be treated as historical, not current. The 2025 U.S. affidavit/court-file excerpt for 2:25-mj-30673-DUTY reports court-authorized PRTT records associated with an account, group, participants, approximate 125 communications and transmission date/type data. The actual underlying company return is not public; the complaint’s message excerpts could derive from other sources. The government guide confirms prospective metadata collection, while saying normal operations do not retain messages after delivery or transaction logs; this is not contradictory if the PRTT order triggers targeted collection. It does **not** establish that WhatsApp handed over E2EE plaintext in that matter. [WhatsApp LE guide FR](https://faq.whatsapp.com/444002211197967/?category=5245250&locale=fr_FR), [DOJ case record](https://www.justice.gov/usao-edmi/media/1416961/dl).

#### WhatsApp — matrice des métadonnées

| Métadonnée | Fournisseur Meta / WhatsApp | Contact | ISP | Apple / Google | Autorités |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IP | CONFIRMÉ service sees; PRTT can collect prospective targeted IP/time | POSSIBLE direct 1:1 call | CONFIRMÉ network sees | POSSIBLE store/push/IP account separate | CONFIRMÉ where valid PRTT/record exists; exact log interval unknown |
| Téléphone | CONFIRMÉ in legacy account; usernames may alter contact presentation | CONFIRMÉ or POSSIBLE by privacy/new username feature | CONFIRMÉ carrier knows subscriber/SIM, not app chat | POSSIBLE by store account separate | POSSIBLE/CONFIRMÉ as subscriber info if request |
| Email | POSSIBLE account/support/backup/business | POSSIBLE profile | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN | CONFIRMÉ store identity if supplied | POSSIBLE if retained/account-linked |
| Identifiant | CONFIRMÉ account/device IDs and phone | CONFIRMÉ profile/name/number as shown | POSSIBLE traffic correlation | CONFIRMÉ app install/device/account signals | CONFIRMÉ basic subscriber records and device info when available |
| Graphe social | PROBABLE contacts/delivery/groups; WhatsApp says not routinely logging who everyone messages | CONFIRMÉ conversation participants | POSSIBLE traffic correlation | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN except app-store usage | CONFIRMÉ in documented PRTT case for a group/participants |
| Timestamps | PROBABLE delivery/session metadata; targeted PRTT confirms time | CONFIRMÉ message/call time | CONFIRMÉ IP session/time | POSSIBLE push/install events | CONFIRMÉ targeted PRTT time; normal history retention varies |
| Volumes | POSSIBLE/PROBABLE store/metadata; exact fields unknown | CONFIRMÉ message counts/media size | CONFIRMÉ bytes | POSSIBLE app diagnostics | PROBABLE from record types; underlying disclosure not fully public |
| Groupes | CONFIRMÉ membership/routing data may be returned; policy/account info can include groups | CONFIRMÉ group members | POSSIBLE only traffic patterns | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN by store | CONFIRMÉ group data referenced in 2025 PRTT affidavit |
| Appareils | CONFIRMÉ linked devices / device type | POSSIBLE linked sessions/status | POSSIBLE fingerprint | CONFIRMÉ store device association | POSSIBLE device/basic account fields under process |
| Push tokens | PROBABLE APNs/FCM and Meta delivery backend | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN to peer | NON ACCESSIBLE PAR DESIGN | CONFIRMÉ token holder provider, exact token retention unknown | POSSIBLE via Meta/provider process |

## Discord — dossier individuel d’investigation (13 octobre 2026)

**Périmètre.** Discord messages/text, server channels and DMs are not E2EE. As of March 2026 DAVE E2EE is on by default for eligible voice/video calls, excluding Stage channels. A/V keying and message-store semantics are distinct.

| Catégorie | FACT | VENDOR CLAIM | INDEPENDENT EVIDENCE | INFERENCE | UNKNOWN |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Architecture | Centralized account/platform, API, text message storage, servers, media gateway/SFU; most voice/video has Cloudflare Edge routing by 2026. | Discord publishes architecture/engineering notes and DAVE code/spec. | 2026 engineering post states >80% voice/video traffic on Cloudflare Edge network, 300+ cities; specifically voice/video, not all backend. | Platform/operator can read server-side text and group membership; call relays still see network-level metadata. | Full domain/API/region/CDN mapping for text, attachments and each voice path. |
| 2 Protocole cryptographique | DAVE uses MLS-based group keying and ratcheted media encryption; voice gateway distributes/authenticates group-state and SFU relays encrypted media. | Vendor calls DAVE E2EE and says all eligible calls are E2EE by default since early March 2026. | Independent Trail of Bits protocol-design/implementation/WebAssembly reviews; code open. Oct 2026 advisory found a validation bug that could admit unrecognized participant when attacker controls signaling path. | E2EE applies media, not account metadata, membership exposed to signaling, or Discord text. | Current rollouts/platform protocol versions, client/server adoption of libdave fix v1.2.0, cross-platform consistency. |
| 3 Création d’identité | Account uses unique username/user ID plus credentials and may add email/phone; device/account telemetry. | Discord offers pseudonymous display identities and safety controls. | Privacy policy and LE guide enumerate subscriber/profile information. | Stable user ID and server/usernames can be correlated across public messages even if no legal name. | Which signup credential is mandatory per geography/product flow and retention. |
| 4 Contact discovery | Friend lookup via username/user ID, invites and contact sync/phone features depending settings. | Discord says contact sync is optional and controllable. | Help/policy public; no current app permission/traffic test. | Searchable unique handles and public server participation are OSINT surfaces. | Exact current contact-upload scheme/rate limits and account visibility defaults. |
| 5 Métadonnées serveur | User/account, IP, device, messages, interactions, timestamps, server/channel membership, attachments and event logs may be held; text DMs/channels not E2EE. | Privacy policy and transparency explain collection/retention/requests. | DOJ case in Oct 2026 shows stored DM records actually returned under warrant; case cites voluntary Discord data too. | Central service can produce message body/metadata subject to retention and legal process. | Exact retention by data type, deleted messages, audit access, region-specific logging. |
| 6 IP utilisateur | Discord receives source IP for API/login; voice/Cloudflare edge sees IP per session; policy lists IP. | Discord claims use for service/security and describes subprocessors. | Engineering post confirms Cloudflare edge voice traffic; policy/LE guide. | More than one provider may observe a call path; Cloudflare does not become E2EE endpoint but sees network/session. | Per-product logs/retention and mapping of text API to hosts/ASNs. |
| 7 IP au correspondant | Calls use relayed service infrastructure, voice Gateway/SFU; peer-to-peer exposure is not generally required as a Discord call architecture. | DAVE docs describe SFU/relay voice design. | Public architecture and edge migration post; no pcap or browser ICE candidate test here. | Discord relay can mask peer IP but sees each endpoint IP; bots/links outside voice may expose separately. | Exact clients/modes using direct vs relay candidates, stage/stream exception. |
| 8 Messages | Text DMs/server content is server-readable and stored subject to policy; no E2EE for text as of reviewed product docs. | Discord says it stores/processes content for service, safety, moderation and user controls. | DOJ US v Bryant says Discord gave FBI responsive records from a warrant; messages absent from suspect’s phone. This is strong case evidence text was available at server. | DAVE E2EE must not be generalized to messages. | Retention/deletion behavior and if future private message E2EE features alter scope. |
| 9 Groupes | DMs, group DMs, server channels with roles/membership; chat messages unencrypted E2EE-wise. | Discord moderation/safety features support scanning/review/reporting. | Court case records DMs obtained; public API/product semantics. | Server operator knows membership, message and role graph; members may copy contents. | Exact archived/deleted message retention and legal fields per channel. |
| 10 Appels | Eligible voice/video DMs, group DMs, voice channels, Go Live are DAVE E2EE by default since early Mar 2026; Stage channels excluded. | Discord says DAVE covers audio/video and prevents platform access to media plaintext. | ToB scoped reviews plus open protocol/code and 2026 vendor engineering statement; no runtime capture across every platform. | SFU/voice gateway still sees IP/participants/timing/control metadata, but E2EE can prevent reading valid media. | Platform/version rollout and whether any bot/SDK/Stage stream interop changes media security. |
| 11 Fichiers | Attachments uploaded to Discord storage/CDN; ordinary chat files are not E2EE at rest/through service in this dossier. | Privacy/safety docs provide upload scanning/moderation descriptions. | Discord court/LE materials and policy; no current file ciphertext test. | Service can access stored attachments, while links/CDN cache can outlive message context. | Encryption at rest/key custody, attachment retention after deletion, CDN and previews. |
| 12 Multi-device | Account login across desktop/mobile/browser/console and OAuth/bots; server synchronizes text. | Discord promotes cross-platform sessions. | Open DAVE library targets client platforms; case/policy support multi-device. | Additional signed-in clients and push tokens expand endpoint and access surfaces. | Session/device revocation semantics, per-device key handling for DAVE and exact sync data. |
| 13 Push | Mobile/desktop push and notification services; preview contents may be locally configurable; exact providers by platform. | Discord exposes notification settings and privacy controls. | No current binary manifest/payload capture. | Apple/Google or OS notification broker sees token/event and may receive notification metadata/content. | FCM/APNs use, payload, SDKs, token retention, lock-screen truncation. |
| 14 Stockage local | Apps cache local DB, attachments, logs and sessions; decrypted text accessible to signed-in app. | Discord has local app settings/cache clearing. | No current app data-dir forensic report tied to 2026 build in this investigation. | Unlocked session or compromised process gives plain text irrespective of DAVE media encryption. | Encryption-at-rest details, OS key store, current schema and cache deletion. |
| 15 Sauvegardes | User export/data package and OS/desktop backup possibilities; no user-managed E2EE backup guarantee for messages established. | Discord offers data request package and user data controls. | No encrypted backup protocol audit found. | Cloud platform copies/server retention are distinct from local backup. | Device backup defaults, export encryption and retention/deletion after account removal. |
| 16 Analytics / SDK tiers | Policy enumerates interactions/diagnostics/device data; analytics settings permit use of data to improve Discord. Third-party customer support vendor incident exposed submitted support tickets and IDs in Oct 2025. | Discord says user controls and subprocessor disclosures apply. | Official breach notice and Play/Apple disclosures; no current binary traffic/SDK scan. | Analytics toggle is not equivalent to preventing core account/IP/session telemetry; contractor may hold support data. | SDK inventory, Firebase/Sentry/analytics runtime payload and retention by app platform. |
| 17 Code source | `discord/dave-protocol` and `discord/libdave` public; primary client, text backend and service infrastructure closed. | Discord says protocol is open and audited. | GitHub repos and ToB public report index; code review does not make full service open. | Public cryptographic component covers limited layer, not deployed clients/server end-to-end. | Exact commits bundled in each current client and server deployment. |
| 18 Builds reproductibles | No full official reproducible build for Discord client established. | DAVE protocol openness/audit should not be read as client rebuild claim. | Closed desktop/mobile app and no matched rebuild evidence. | Store signatures authenticate source channel, not source transparency. | Reproducible client build / provenance attestations / component hashes. |
| 19 Supply chain | Open DAVE code/library depends on release integration; closed app updater, platform store, Cloudflare and other cloud/service providers. CVE fixed in library v1.2.0/cpp commit `9686fba`. | Vendor/advisory recommends patched libdave. | GitHub release and advisory publicly link commit; no independent check of deployed client rollout. | Update distribution and gateway trust are critical to DAVE’s security guarantee. | Exact libdave versions live on all platforms, lockfile/SBOM, release signing and CI secret controls. |
| 20 Infrastructure OSINT | Voice/video edge: Cloudflare 300+ cities, >80% traffic per June 2026 Discord article; prior provider about 30 cities. | Discord engineering post details rollout. | Primary engineering source, not independent traffic measurement. No live DNS/ASN/IP/route query done. | That coverage is only A/V and does not describe chat, DB or file backend. | Domains, current DNS, ASN/anycast, exact regions and cloud providers for each service. |
| 21 Juridiction | Discord Inc. US company; data processed by vendors/regions; legal response depends on user and process. | Policy/LE guide state legal entity and handling. | Public corporate terms and court cases. | US process can reach company, but cross-border restrictions and user location complicate. | Data controller/subprocessor per country and litigation challenges. |
| 22 Demandes policières | Discord publishes legal guide/transparency; can provide subscriber/IP/account/message data according to process and retention. | Discord says reviews legally valid requests and emergencies. | US v Bryant 2026 provides actual disclosure of DM records following warrant; profile identifiers also used in identification. | Unlike E2EE text messenger, content of stored Discord chats can be remitted. | Per-case field set, disclosure counts, retention windows and gag-order rates. |
| 23 Affaires judiciaires connues | **U.S. v James Cody Bryant, 2:26-mj-00618-BAT, complaint filed 2 Oct 2026:** Discord voluntarily gave RCMP account information; later FBI warrant dated 13 Mar 2026 and records delivered 26 Mar 2026; many DMs over preceding six months, not present on Bryant’s phone. | — | DOJ affidavit/complaint states facts directly, primary court document. It includes account subscriber, communications, usernames, dates, messages; Discord’s own reported review tied account to user. | Actual platform message contents/records obtained; no E2EE claim applies to DMs. Still a complaint affidavit, not a final judgment on guilt. | Exact raw export/metadata fields and whether complete archive included. |
| 24 Audits | Three Trail of Bits reports read directly: DAVE design (PDF cover 16 Nov. 2023; ToB index row says Aug. 2024, 4 engineer-weeks), DAVE code review (final 16 Sep. 2024; 5 engineer-weeks), WebAssembly integration (3 Jun. 2025; 3 engineer-weeks). Targets, commits, findings, scope and fix-review text are transcribed below. | Discord presents DAVE as audited; that is a scoped claim, not assurance about all clients, server systems or later revisions. | The full public PDFs establish 11 design findings, 15 implementation findings and one non-security code-quality note; design and implementation reports include fix reviews with an internal inconsistency in the implementation report. | Exact source revision/current deployed builds and independent confirmation of each remediation remain separate questions. | Whether production clients use the reviewed/fixed revisions on every platform; independent retest after the Oct. 2026 advisory. |
| 25 Vulnérabilités | CVE-2026-104480 / GHSA-3q99-x36r-rchh (Oct 2 2026): libdave before 1.2.0 failed to reject MLS Welcome with unrecognized member; attacker must control DAVE signaling path or equivalent. Fix release v1.2.0/cpp, commit `9686fba`; affected/deployed ranges unknown. | Advisory describes patch; vendor claims no full client rollout? (No deployment statement verified.) | GHSA/NVD and signed GitHub release are public; finding attacks media confidentiality/integrity but requires signaling-path control. CVSS 9.4 indicates severity, not proof exploited. | Gateway integrity is part of real-world E2EE trust boundary; a remote arbitrary Discord user without signaling control is not shown to exploit it. | Affected production-client versions, exploitation in wild, rollout to all platforms and independent regression test. |
| 26 Recherche académique | MLS/DAVE analyses and audits are public; no independent academic measurement of current text moderation/log behavior found. | DAVE design paper reports its model. | Trail of Bits reports plus open IETF MLS docs. | Protocol assurance does not characterize platform deployment access or abuse. | Peer-reviewed 2026 DAVE security evaluation and independent client model. |
